# Parallels index: the Egyptian corpus (`חכמה-ובריאה-במצרים/`)

## Key findings (Round 4 run 3, 2026-10-24)

The full table (805 rows) follows, by Bible verse. The strongest contacts:

1. **Amenemope ↔ Prov 22:17–24:22, saying by saying, checked on the transliteration** (renderings §8): "give your ears"
   (AM 3.9) against `הַט אָזְנְךָ` – a [divergence] the English hides; "the casket of your belly" against `בְּבִטְנֶךָ` (the
   oldest witness, oCairo SR 1840, has only "inside your belly"); the hot man (11.13–14, 13.8–9) and Prov 22:24–25 in
   the same order; wealth that "made itself wings like geese and flew to the sky" (9.10–10.5) against `כִּי עָשֹׂה יַעֲשֶׂה-לּוֹ
   כְנָפַיִם כְּנֶשֶׁר` – same verb and noun, another bird; the thirty houses (27.7) behind the qere `שָׁלִשִׁים` of Prov 22:20.
2. **The Torah's laws of the boundary, the measure and the weak in an Egyptian surveyor's mouth:** the boundary stone
   and the widow's boundary (7.12–15 ↔ Deut 19:14, 27:17, Prov 23:10 – the emendation `גְּבוּל אַלְמָנָה`); two words for the
   balance, as in Prov 16:11 `פֶּלֶס וּמֹאזְנֵי` (17.18–18.3); the oipe "that takes two" (18.21–19.1) ↔ `אֵיפָה וְאֵיפָה`
   (Deut 25:14) – `אֵיפָה` is the Egyptian word itself, and the text calls the false measure the god's "abomination"
   (`תּוֹעֵבָה`); the blind, the dwarf and the lame (24.9–12 ↔ Lev 19:14); the widow in the field and the stranger
   (26.9–13 ↔ Deut 24:19–21); "man is clay and straw, the god his builder" (24.12–17 ↔ Gen 2:7, Job 33:6).
3. **"Not yet" (Gen 2:4b–5):** the Pyramid Texts' `n ḫpr.t X` (PT571, PT486) is the exact frame of `טֶרֶם יִהְיֶה` – and in
   Egypt death too "came into being" (Gen 2:17 [divergence]).
4. **The Memphite Theology:** creation by heart and tongue, the mouth "that devised the name of every thing" (Gen 2:20,
   Ps 33:6, 9); "Ptah rested (ḥtp) after he had made everything" (59) – `נָח` as Ex 20:11 `וַיָּנַח`, with no day and no
   number (Gen 2:2 [divergence]); "his work" (kꜣ.t `מְלָאכָה`, Gen 2:2); a worm-eaten ancestral book recopied by Shabaka
   (Prov 25:1: Hezekiah was his contemporary).
5. **Merikare (E130–138):** mankind as "the god's flock" and "his images" for whom he made heaven, earth, breath, plants,
   birds and fish (Gen 1:26–30, 2:7) – and who punished only the plotters among them (Gen 6:5–7 [divergence]).
6. **The Heavenly Cow ↔ Gen 6–9, 11** (every reserved root of the flood story kept out of the lines): the god's change
   of heart is weariness, not regret (Gen 6:6 [divergence]); mercy by a trick of red beer, not by a chosen survivor;
   "never again" (Gen 8:21) and the separation of heaven and earth (Gen 11); `jw=j jm=j` "I (am) I" (49) against Ex 3:14
   (Hornung's "Ich bin, der ich bin" adds a verb and a relative the Egyptian lacks); Thoth installed "in my place" (Gen
   41:40).
7. **Loanwords seen in their own contexts:** `תֵּבָה` (AM 11.4, the coffin), `אֵיפָה`, `חָתַם` (sin "sealed with his finger",
   19.21 ↔ Job 14:17), `יְאֹר`, `כֶּתֶם`; Semitic loans in Late Egyptian (ꜥšq `עָשַׁק`, mkmr.t `מִכְמֹרֶת`).

**The ancient mind:** a land surveyor's conscience (sins and punishments of his trade); the "hot" and the "silent" man as
two trees; measures as divine eyes (the Udjat, the Eye of Re) and Thoth's baboon at every market balance; a creator who
withdraws out of old age; creation as thought, word and pun; magic as a gift of god; the uncreated serpent destroyed
every day (the fifth-day dossier's Westcar and Bremner-Rhind excerpts, `../מיתוסים-אוגריתיים/3-תיק-היום-החמישי/`).

## Key findings (Round 5 run 6, 2026-11-01: the tales and BD 125)
- **Gen 39 ↔ the Two Brothers** (TB 3,7–5,4): the proposal "come, let us lie" in sḏr (`שכב`); the refusal grounded in kinship ("like a mother, like a father") where Joseph invokes his master's trust and God [divergence]; "this great offence" (4,1 ↔ `הָרָעָה הַגְּדֹלָה הַזֹּאת`, without "sin against God"); the wife stages a beaten body, not a garment in her hand, and demands his death [divergence]; here the wife is punished (8,8 ↔ 39:20 reversed).
- **Gen 2:18, 22 ↔ TB 9,6–9,7:** the gods pity the lonely man and Khnum is told to "build" (qd `בנה`) him a wife [equivalent].
- **Ex 7:8–12, 14:21–22 ↔ Westcar:** the lector priest (`חַרְטֹם`, the loanword) and the wax crocodile (the dossier's WC); Djadjaemankh lays half the lake's water on the other half to recover a girl's pendant – folded, not split, and for a trinket [divergence]; Djedi's 110 years (Gen 50:26); the refusal to test death on a man, "the noble flock of the god" (8,16–8,17 ↔ Gen 9:6, Ps 100:3); Re's sons born with midwife goddesses (Ex 1:15–21 [divergence]).
- **BD 125 ↔ Ps 15, 24:3–6, Deut 26:13–14, Job 31, the Decalogue:** forty-two denials before named judges at the gate of a hall; "I have not transgressed" thj ↔ `לֹא עָבַרְתִּי` (Deut 26:13); bread to the hungry, a garment to the naked (71 ↔ Ezek 18:7, Isa 58:7); the heart weighed by "the perceiver of hearts, the searcher of bellies" (98–99 ↔ Jer 17:10); the Decalogue's deeds in the ordinary verbs, never its own words.
- **Deut 8:7–9 ↔ Sinuhe B 81–84:** `tꜣ pw nfr` = `אֶרֶץ טוֹבָה`; figs, grapes, wine, honey, barley, emmer – the list overlaps four species, without wheat, pomegranate or "milk and honey"; Sinuhe's flight and marriage to the nomad chief's daughter (Ex 2:15–21), the duel (1 Sam 17), and burial at home reversed (Gen 47:30, 50:25: Egypt is the homeland).
- **The Shipwrecked Sailor ↔ Jonah 1–2, Job 1:16, Ps 107:** a lone survivor, a great creature that takes him in its mouth and returns him; fire from heaven burns the serpent's household, "I alone escaped".

| בראשית ב:יז | PT571 P/V/W.35 | n ḫpr.t p,t n ḫpr.t tꜣ n ḫpr.t r(m)ṯ.pl n msi̯.t nṯr.pl n ḫpr.t m(w)t nhj Ppy pn hrw ẖr(,j) m(w)t mr nhi̯.t Stš hrw =f ẖr(,j) m(w)t jw Ppy pn jr jdr.pl? =ṯn nṯr.pl nn,t(j).w j:ḫm.w n(j)k n ḫft(,j).w =sn n n(j)k Ppy pn n ḫft(,j).w =f | טרם נהיו השמים, טרם נהייתה הארץ, טרם נהיו בני האדם, טרם נולדו הָאֵלִים, טרם נהיה המוות. יימלט פפי זה מן היום אשר תחת המוות, כהימלט סֵת מיומו אשר תחת המוות. פפי זה – לעדריכם?, אֵלִים אשר בשמי־המטה, אשר אינם מכירים עונש בגלל אויביהם; לא ייענש פפי זה בגלל אויביו. | `כִּי בְּיוֹם אֲכָלְךָ מִמֶּנּוּ מוֹת תָּמוּת` (בראשית ב:יז) | divergence | בבראשית המוות בא לעולם בגזרה, אחרי הבריאה ובעקבות מעשה האדם; כאן המוות הוא דבר ש"נהיה" כמו השמים והארץ, ומי שנולד לפניו יכול להימלט ממנו |
| בראשית ב:יח | TB 9,6 | n =f r-jqr zp-2 jw pꜣ Rꜥ-Ḥr,w-ꜣḫ,t.du ḥr ḏd n H̱nm,w jḫ qd =k wꜥ-n s,t-ḥm,t n Bꜣ~tʾ tm ={k}⟨f⟩ | עליו מאוד מאוד. ורַא־חַרְאַחְתִּי אמר לחְנוּם: "תבנה אפוא אשה לבטה, שלא | `לֹא טוֹב הֱיוֹת הָאָדָם לְבַדּוֹ אֶעֱשֶׂה לּוֹ עֵזֶר כְּנֶגְדּוֹ` (בראשית ב:יח) | equivalent | הנימוק: שלא ישב לבדו (9,7); ושם `אֶעֱשֶׂה` כמו כאן `עשה` (9,7) |

| בראשית ב:כג | PT600 N/F/E.inf.17 | ḏ(d)-mdw (J)tm(,w) Ḫprr qꜣi̯.n =k m qꜣꜣ wbn.n =k m bnbn m Ḥw,t-bn,w m Jwn,w jšš.n =k m Šw tfn.(n) =k m Tfn,t di̯.n =k ꜥ.du =k ḥꜣ =sn(j) m ꜥ kꜣ wn kꜣ =k | דְּבָרִים לֵאמֹר: אָתוּם־חֶפְּרִי, גָּבַהְתָּ כַּגִּבְעָה, זרחת כַּבֶּנְבֶּן בְּבֵית־הַבֶּנוּ בְּאוֹן; עָטַשְׁתָּ את שׁוּ, יָרַקְתָּ את תֶּפְנוּת, שמת את זרועותיך סביבם כִּזְרוֹעַ־כָּא – יהיה כָּאֲךָ | `לְזֹאת יִקָּרֵא אִשָּׁה כִּי מֵאִישׁ לֻקֳחָה זֹּאת` (בראשית ב:כג) | equivalent | שם שנקבע במשחק מלים על מוצאו: *jšš* ~ שו, *tfn* ~ תפנות, כמו `אִשָּׁה` ~ `אִישׁ` |
| בראשית ב:כג | PT600 N/F/E.inf.22 | m ḥri̯ jr =f m rn =f n(,j) mḥr km.t wr.t m rn =k n(,j) Ḥw,t-Km-wr di̯.n n =k Ḏḥw,tj nṯr.pl ẖr =k ḏꜣi̯.w mꜣꜥ.w m dḏꜣ m dmꜣꜥ Ḥr,w m jt(j) =k (W)sr(,w) m rn =f n(,j) Ḥw,t-jt(j) | אל תתרחק ממנו בשמו "פירמידה". שחור אתה, גדול אתה, בשמך "בית־השחור־הגדול". שם לך תְּחוּת את הָאֵלִים תחתיך, *צולחים ומונהגים בִּדְגָ׳א, בִּדְמָאע*?. חורוס, הנה אביך אוסיריס, בשמו "בית־הריבון". | `לְזֹאת יִקָּרֵא אִשָּׁה כִּי מֵאִישׁ לֻקֳחָה זֹּאת` (בראשית ב:כג) | equivalent | שם שנגזר במשחק מלים, כמו "שחור" / "בית־השחור־הגדול". |
